# Skills · I · part 37

868 entries, in-silico-read-simulation to inbox-triage. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **in-silico-read-simulation**: Use when benchmarking a metagenomic assembler or contig extender and a ground-truth-controlled read set is needed spanning known read lengths, error rates, coverage depths, and uneven coverage profiles. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/in-silico-read-simulation))
- **in-silico-saturated-mutagenesis-analysis**: Use when you need to dissect which bases and sequence motifs around a non-coding SNP drive a predicted chromatin activity change (e.g., transcription factor binding) for a single variant, at single-base resolution. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/in-silico-saturated-mutagenesis-analysis))
- **in-silico-scrnaseq-mixture-assembly**: Use when benchmarking single-cell genetic mixture deconvolution (e.g. de-goulash) against controlled imbalance levels, by pooling barcoded cells from two known scRNA-seq donors at defined ratios such as 1:10–1:99. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/in-silico-scrnaseq-mixture-assembly))
- **in-silico-sensitivity-analysis-of-rule-inference**: Use when you need to determine whether scBONITA Boolean rule determination is robust to the size of a cluster's scRNA-seq training data, or to choose how many cells a training subset must contain before inference degrades. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/in-silico-sensitivity-analysis-of-rule-inference))
- **in-silico-single-cell-data-simulation**: Use when a single-cell computational method needs validation against a known ground truth (e.g., a known gene-regulatory network with planted fate-driving TFs) before applying it to real scRNA-seq data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/in-silico-single-cell-data-simulation))
- **in-silico-single-cell-mixture-construction**: Use when benchmarking deconvolution or coupled clustering of bulk/single-cell RNA-seq, ATAC-seq, and HiChIP data and true subpopulation-specific profiles are needed as ground truth for computing PCC against predicted profiles. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/in-silico-single-cell-mixture-construction))
- **in-silico-single-cell-simulation**: Use when you need simulated single-cell RNA-seq data that preserves gene identities, cell trajectories, pseudo-time ordering, and noise of a reference dataset, or ground-truth GRNs for benchmarking GRN inference. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/in-silico-single-cell-simulation))
- **in-silico-single-gene-deletion-analysis**: Use when you have a metabolic model (e.g., a Reconstructor-generated SBML GENRE like NCGM2.S1.xml) and need to identify which genes are essential versus non-essential for biomass production. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/in-silico-single-gene-deletion-analysis))
- **in-silico-single-gene-deletion-analysis**: Use when you have context-specific genome-scale metabolic models (e.g. built with rFASTCORMICS on Recon2.04/Recon3D_01) with a VBOF and need to find genes essential to viral biomass but not host growth. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/in-silico-single-gene-deletion-analysis))
- **in-silico-spectra-exclusion**: Use when compiling a training set from public libraries (MoNA, GNPS, LipidMaps) while a separate annotation reference (NIST 20, LipidBlast) will score the evaluation set, to prevent overlap. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/in-silico-spectra-exclusion))
- **in-silico-spectral-library-benchmarking**: Use when comparing two or more in-silico lipid fragmentation libraries by enumerating per-class and per-adduct library entry counts and feature annotation counts to reveal coverage gaps such as classes unique to one library. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/in-silico-spectral-library-benchmarking))
- **in-silico-spectrum-generation-cfmid**: Use when when you have a list of SMILES strings representing chemical structures and need to create paired SMILES-spectrum training data for a generative model (like MSGO) without requiring experimental mass spectra. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/in-silico-spectrum-generation-cfmid) · 2 copies)
- **in-silico-spectrum-metadata-curation**: Use when when processing collections of in-silico mass spectra from OMSLs (Open Mass Spectra Libraries) where the adduct field is absent, null, or not explicitly specified in the source file; ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/in-silico-spectrum-metadata-curation))
- **in-silico-spectrum-validation**: Use when you need to benchmark a machine-learned (e.g., NEIMS) EI-MS predictor against experimental spectra before trusting its outputs for library building or matching. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/in-silico-spectrum-validation))
- **in-silico-structure-annotation-integration**: Use when a GNPS molecular network (classical or feature-based) contains nodes lacking chemical class labels and you have tab-separated annotation tables with Scan and SMILES columns from library hits, NAP, or DEREPLICATOR to propagate ClassyFire taxonomy onto the network. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/in-silico-structure-annotation-integration))
- **in-silico-structure-classification-evaluation**: Use when a ConCISE consensus annotation run must be validated: compare consensus superclass/class/subclass assignments against GNPS-reported compound identities to compute true positive rates and check the &gt;95% criterion. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/in-silico-structure-classification-evaluation))
- **in-silico-substructure-fragmentation**: Use when you need to rank candidate molecules for an unknown metabolite from accurate-mass MS/MS peak lists without reference spectra, e.g. candidate lists from PubChem for CASMI-style contests or untargeted LC-MS identification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/in-silico-substructure-fragmentation))
- **in-silico-tf-knockout-simulation**: Use when scRNA-seq data from a reprogramming or differentiation process has state-annotated cells and inferred GRNs, and you need to rank candidate TF regulators by simulating knockouts before experimental validation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/in-silico-tf-knockout-simulation--84bfe0efe0e9d916))
- **in-silico-tf-knockout-simulation**: Use when you have a trained GRouNdGAN checkpoint and an imposed (e.g., GRNBoost2- or PPCOR-derived) bipartite TF-gene GRN, and you want to test whether knocking out each TF significantly alters its target genes in simulated cells. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/in-silico-tf-knockout-simulation--ef93d7fd912404a6))
- **in-silico-transcription-factor-perturbation-simulation**: Use when CellOracle in silico TF perturbation simulations must be converted into quantitative perturbation scores to rank candidate regulators (e.g., Gata1 KO on the Paul et al. 2015 hematopoiesis data) for experimental validation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/in-silico-transcription-factor-perturbation-simulation))
- **in-silico-tryptic-digestion**: Use when a simulated gold-standard LC-MS/MS dataset is needed: a known FASTA proteome must be converted into a finite, size-controlled peptide list so that identification recovery (PSMs at 1% q-value) can be measured against ground truth. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/in-silico-tryptic-digestion))
- **in-source-fragment-adduct-candidate-flagging**: Use when you have aligned, imputed time-resolved mass spectrometric data from direct-injection plasma ionization (e.g., DBDI, DESI) without chromatographic separation, and you suspect multiple m/z features belong to the same neutral analyte as in-source fragments or oxygen-bound adducts. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/in-source-fragment-adduct-candidate-flagging))
- **in-source-fragment-and-adduct-annotation**: Use when working with untargeted, time-resolved direct-injection (e.g. DBDI-FT-ICR-MS) MS data lacking chromatographic separation, and multiple ion species from one analyte (adducts/in-source fragments) must be putatively identified. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/in-source-fragment-and-adduct-annotation))
- **in-source-fragment-annotation**: Use when processing raw LC-MS (mzML or Bruker .d) nontargeted metabolomics data and you need to collapse features into groups annotated as isotopes, adducts, or in-source fragments to remove false-positive features. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/in-source-fragment-annotation))
- **in-source-fragment-correlation-clustering**: Use when input is full-scan (MS1-only) LC-MS data in mzML/mzXML — e.g., files from GNPS/MassIVE lacking MS/MS — and the goal is to generate pseudo MS/MS spectra from in-source fragments for library-based structural annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/in-source-fragment-correlation-clustering))
- **in-source-fragment-identification**: Use when you have an LCMS feature table (from XCMS, MS-DIAL, MZmine2, or other feature extraction software) with m/z, retention time, and intensity columns, plus MS2 spectral annotations from DDA data, and you need to identify which features are in-source fragments rather than distinct metabolites. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/in-source-fragment-identification) · 2 copies)
- **in-source-fragment-identification-lcms**: Use when an LC-MS/MS metabolomics feature table contains spurious features suspected to be in-source fragments, and you need to flag ISF features and their precursor-fragment relationships from mzXML/feature-table inputs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/in-source-fragment-identification-lcms))
- **in-source-fragment-matching-to-msms**: Use when annotating LC/HRMS peaks from MS1-only or DDA/DIA untargeted metabolomics data where MS2 fragmentation spectra are absent or sparse, and you want to leverage in-source fragments within composite spectra as surrogates for MS/MS library matches. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/in-source-fragment-matching-to-msms))
- **in-source-fragment-recognition**: Use when processing untargeted LC-MS/MS metabolomics data (e.g. DDA/DIA/fullscan mzXML from NIST plasma-style acquisitions) where ISF features may be misannotated as real metabolites. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/in-source-fragment-recognition))
- **in-source-fragment-rule-matching**: Use when per-sample MGF clean consensus spectra from LC–MS/MS contain multiple ionization variants of one precursor (in-source fragments, adducts, dimers) that must be collapsed to a single [M + H]+ ion entity before molecular networking. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/in-source-fragment-rule-matching))
- **in-source-spectrum-reconstruction**: Use when a RAMClustR-clustered feature table (post-XCMS MS and idMS/MS features with cluster memberships and a feature-by-sample abundance matrix) must be converted into deconvoluted spectra for spectral matching or annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/in-source-spectrum-reconstruction))

- **in-the-loop**: Load at the start of every task when several agents, chats or sessions work for the same person or team, and again before reviewing, deploying, reporting or changing anything shared. Each agent reads a shared live board (what the others just did: versions deployed, settings changed, decisions, what the user is working on right now) before acting, and writes one line to it after any change that others should know about. Stops the "I was reviewing the old version" and "I didn't know you already changed that" mistakes. Agents stay informed without stepping into each other's work.
